Server-side instrumentation (a temporary route console.log + the standalone server booted on the e2e port so reuseExistingServer adopts it + a query-param state dump on the suspect fetch) captured `PUT listLen=1` then `PUT ?dbgEls=0&dbgSave=unsaved listLen=0` — the keepalive persisting a CLOBBERED EMPTY store. Root cause: Next 14.1+ integrates window.history.replaceState into the App Router — the Untitled adoption's replaceState re-ran the load effect mid-autosave; the re-run's GET raced the machine's PUT; the unconditional loadProject clobbered the live store; the keepalive wiped the just-saved element. Fixes: the loader's adoption-clobber guard (skip the re-load when the store already holds the target project) + the keepalive's flushing guard REMOVED (a regular in-flight fetch does not survive teardown). - Empirical verification, not just reading: prisma db push proven to CREATE the missing db/ parent (fresh-clone flow safe); e2e.db isolation confirmed on disk (<repo>/db/e2e.db vs custom.db, no stray out-of-repo db); the seed's pristine counts re-verified (1/2/6/1/3); `git ls-files db/` checked (db binaries untracked as documented).
- Focus areas verified intact: the elements PUT's ELEMENT_LIMIT import + full-list replace inside the interactive transaction with the in-tx response read (S56-H L-2 holds), the POST ceiling (S60-D), the pre-validation type loop (L-1), the field clamps; the AI route's degrade-not-fail + sanitizeLlmOperations boundary (typed vocabulary, 3-or-6 hex, 50-op cap) with locked-delete enforcement at the client seam (S27-1); the teams email seam (S60-C — both invite paths share the identical regex + 400); the no-enumeration forgot-password 200 + relative resetUrl + token-before-password + single-use clear; safeFromUrl on both login consumers; the XFF-bucket e2e budget respected by auth.spec.

Stage Summary:
- 0 Critical / 0 High / 2 Medium / 9 Low / 4 Informational. Medium: (1) clientIpOf trusts the FIRST x-forwarded-for hop — spoofable both directly and behind the code's own "single trusted proxy" assumption, so the documented 10/IP/15min brute-force defense is client-evadable (the e2e suite's own XFF-bucket rotation demonstrates the bypass); (2) verify-otp's wrong-code counter is a non-atomic read-modify-write — concurrent wrong guesses collapse the count, weakening the documented 5-attempt ceiling (compounding finding 1: a concurrent guess loop against a fixed 6-digit code on an unverified account, which verify-otp turns into an open session). Low set: Math.random verify-code generators with a comment claiming crypto-random (×3 sites), resend-otp's account-existence/state leak (409 vs 400 vs 200) + login timing oracle, unbounded name/email/password/request-body lengths on the public register route, PATCH-vs-POST name-cap drift (400 vs silent truncate), duplicate route's name exceeding the 120 cap + non-transactional copy, the dead thumbnailSeed schema column, P2025/P2003 races escaping the {ok,error} envelope on concurrent project delete (the S56-H class, unhandled in the tx), GET /api/projects hauling full element payloads (fillImage data URLs) on every list view, and the POST-vs-PUT fill/stroke default asymmetry (omitted fill→blue vs null) with ~30 duplicated clamp lines begging one shared row-builder. Informational: the ADR-014 in-response OTP/reset-token delivery (account proof = knowing the email — production must swap delivery first), the ADR-003 single-role shared Project/Team pool (no userId — the multi-user seam to revisit), the AI elementSummary prompt-hygiene note (currently enum/geometry-only, no injectable free text), and the unused case-sensitive ?search=/?template= server params. Every finding cites code; the documented contracts (per-process limiter, HMAC cookie design, exact-match proxy redirects, in-response code delivery, single-role data model) were deliberately NOT reported as defects.

Findings:
- LOW (doc integrity — S79-C follow-on miss): the doctrine files still describe the removed tsconfig flag. AGENTS.md:116 "- **TypeScript is strict except `noImplicitAny: false`** (sandbox default; kept intentionally)" and CLAUDE.md:46 "Strict mode is on EXCEPT `noImplicitAny: false` (sandbox default, kept intentionally)" — both stale: tsconfig.json:11 carries only `"strict": true` (grep noImplicitAny → zero hits; tsc --noEmit green, re-run this audit). docs/remediation-plan-session79.md:59 itself says "the compensating-control claim in AGENTS.md becomes fully true" — the claim text was never edited. A session reading AGENTS.md fresh will believe implicit-any is sanctioned. Third instance of the same family: CLAUDE.md:93 says "253 Playwright checks" while CLAUDE.md:100 and AGENTS.md:22 say "256 e2e".
- LOW (pin quality / S79-D): the anti-clickjacking headers are pinned ONLY as source text — tests/server-lows-s79.test.ts:96-121 is five `expect(config).toMatch(...)` regexes over next.config.ts content; scripts/smoke-test.sh (430 lines, 58 checks) and every e2e spec assert ZERO response headers (grep X-Frame/nosniff/Referrer across smoke + e2e → only the s79 unit file). next.config.ts:43-44 claims "The standalone build honors next.config headers" — true today (live-verified above) but asserted by no gate; a future regression (config restructure, manifest change) would pass the full six-gate ladder silently. Three `curl -sI | grep` lines in the smoke suite would close it.
- LOW (security, documented-posture edge with an uncalled-out asymmetry): resend-otp delivers the fresh verification code to ANY caller who knows the email — no ownership proof. src/app/api/auth/resend-otp/route.ts:16-79: inputs are only `email` + the IP rate limit; line 78 `return ok({ verificationCode: inAppOtpEnabled ? verifyCode : null })`; src/app/api/auth/verify-otp/route.ts:82-104 then opens the session on that code — the chain (resend → verify) takes over any UNVERIFIED account pre-verification. Contrast the sibling sites: login's unverified branch (login/route.ts:67-92) regenerates the code only AFTER verifyPassword succeeds; register delivers only to the registrant. Documented at docs/DEPLOYMENT.md:61-66 ("hands the reset URL / OTP code to any caller that knows an account's email ... set BOTH delivery knobs") — the docs frame it as public-deploy hygiene; the login-password-gated vs resend-email-only ownership asymmetry is not called out anywhere. DIGMA_DISABLE_IN_APP_OTP=1 (resend-otp/route.ts:77) closes the payload half.
- INFO (documented posture): single-tenant data model — no per-user scoping anywhere. prisma/schema.prisma:52-63 (Project: no userId/owner), :114-123 (Team); every route gates on session only (e.g. src/app/api/projects/route.ts:27-35 findMany with no user filter; DELETE removes any id). Documented at docs/DEPLOYMENT.md:65-67 ("any registered account reads/mutates the whole workspace — the reference app's own model ... the only documented posture") and next.config.ts:42-43.
- INFO (documented default): the rate limiter's default proxy trust is fail-open under direct exposure. src/lib/rate-limit.ts:103-109 proxyHopDepth() defaults DIGMA_PROXY_HOPS to 1 (trust XFF/x-real-ip); rate-limit.ts:136-146 clientIpOf — a client rotating X-Forwarded-For/x-real-ip keys a fresh bucket per request and the 10/15min auth + 20/5min AI limits never trip. Documented in .env.example (the DIGMA_PROXY_HOPS block) + DEPLOYMENT.md ("a mis-declared proxy topology can leave [the limiter] bypassable"); the default is the deliberate test-suite-compat choice.
- INFO (reference parity): register's 409 is an account-existence oracle. src/app/api/auth/register/route.ts:50-53 `if (existing) { return fail("CONFLICT", "An account with this email already exists", 409); }` (P2002 twin at :86-88). The no-enumeration contracts cover verify/resend/forgot (resend-otp/route.ts:48-65 answers unknown/verified identically; forgot-password/route.ts:51-86) — register was never in that family (the reference's own measured behavior).
- INFO (documented known edge): the list-family projection still ships full fillImage data-URLs with no response-side bound. src/lib/editor.ts:197-223 THUMBNAIL_ELEMENT_SELECT includes `fillImage: true` (the thumbnail paint needs it — S73-F note); FILL_IMAGE_MAX_CHARS 700_000 × ELEMENT_LIMIT 2000; validation.ts:132-140 documents that "a stored board larger than 32 MB is constructible" via the single-element POST path — every subsequent list GET ships it (× up to PROJECT_LIMIT 500 projects). Authenticated-only, documented.
- INFO: /api/health is the lone route outside the { ok, data } | { ok, error } envelope doctrine. src/app/api/health/route.ts:5-7 `return NextResponse.json({ status: "ok", app: "digma", ts: new Date().toISOString() });` — AGENTS.md's envelope bullet says "every handler". Long-standing, consumed by smoke + the playwright webServer url; never documented as an exemption.
- INFO: buildElementRow's visible/locked accept any truthy JSON value. src/lib/editor.ts:911-912 `visible: raw?.visible === undefined ? true : Boolean(raw?.visible)` / `locked: ... Boolean(raw?.locked)` — a scripted API caller sending `"visible": "false"` (string) or 1 stores `true`; every other field in the seam enforces typeof/enum membership. Client always sends booleans — API-consumer-only impact.
- INFO (documented as queued): no server-side timeout on the LLM await. src/app/api/ai-assistant/route.ts:8 `export const maxDuration = 60;` is a serverless-only hint (ignored by the standalone Node server); the zai.chat.completions.create await (route.ts:95-101) has no AbortController, so a hung SDK call holds the request open past 60s — bounded only by the 20/5min limiter. The session-77 deferral queue documents this exact gap ("the LLM server timeout KEPT QUEUED").

Stage Summary:

- src/app/layout.tsx + all seven page.tsx + globals.css (256) — full; @theme literal-hex audit, v3-opacity/oklch/var()-chain greps clean, no dangerouslySetInnerHTML, window.location used read-only
- Supporting seams read for contract verification: src/lib/editor.ts (915 — boundsOf, fillPaintFor, buildElementRow, isTypingTarget, clampFillImage, TOOL seams), src/lib/call.ts, src/lib/validation.ts (safeFromUrl/normalizeResetToken), src/hooks/use-toast.ts, src/hooks/use-media-query.ts
- S79 pin coverage cross-checked: tests/client-lows-s79.test.ts (262) + tests/e2e/session79-fixes.spec.ts (177) — the e2e's swap-flush probe exercises only the machine-IDLE case (the AI add arms the 800ms timer; the swap lands before it fires)
- Grep/hygiene sweeps: no v3 opacity utilities, no var() chains in @theme, no XSS sinks, React 19 set-state-in-effect discipline clean across every effect (event-callback / awaited-continuation / render-time compare-and-adjust only)

Findings:
- [Medium] S79-B residual race — an edit landing while a flush is IN FLIGHT is still silently lost on a fast same-instance soft swap. editor-view.tsx:156-159 — flushNow() at the swap boundary (editor-view.tsx:1216) hits `if (flushing) { pending = true; return; }` when the 800ms timer's flush is mid-PUT, so NOTHING captures the newer edit (elements_A2). If the incoming project's GET resolves before the outgoing PUT's response, loadProject(B) stamps `saveState: "saved"`; the A-PUT's response is then dropped by the swap guard at editor-view.tsx:261 (`if (capturedProjectId && now.projectId !== capturedProjectId) return;`) BEFORE the elements-reference guard can `setUnsaved`, and the pending re-run in the finally (editor-view.tsx:317-320) early-returns on "saved" (editor-view.tsx:164). A2 is gone from the store, from history (loadProject clears past/future), and was never PUT — unrecoverable, the exact loss class S79-B closed for the machine-idle case (the e2e probe pins only that case: "add 3 circles … pushState-swap inside the window" fires with flushing === false). Fix shape: capture the newer state at the boundary even while flushing (a captured-body cleanup PUT like the unmount path's), or have the swap guard re-arm via a live-refs compare before dropping.
- [Low] S79-A residual — an Untitled→Untitled LOAD (a lineage break with the ""→"" projectId shape) keeps the stale transcript and renders a silently no-op Revert. ai-assistant.tsx:121 — `if (state.projectId === prevState.projectId) return;` fires before the epoch check, so a soft swap /Editor?projectId=unknown1 → /Editor?projectId=unknown2 (both fall to the Untitled fallback, editor-view.tsx:1289-1292) replaces the canvas (boardEpoch moves, elements wiped) while the old conversation survives; a surviving carrier's Revert button still renders (ai-assistant.tsx:475, keyed on revertSnapshot presence only) but the epoch belt (ai-assistant.tsx:271) makes the click a silent no-op — the "a control that lies about its state" / S76-C "a control that cannot work must not render" class. The clobber itself IS defused (the belt holds); the residue is stale transcript + dead chrome. A reset keyed on `state.boardEpoch !== prevState.boardEpoch` (OR the projectId transition) closes it.
- [Low] AI apply: one LLM update op carrying BOTH scale and a paint/geometry patch pushes TWO undo history entries. ai-assistant.tsx:217-223 — `store.scaleElements(targets, ...)` then `store.updateElements(targets, patch)` (default commit=true); scaleElements (editor-store.ts:316-321) and updateElements (editor-store.ts:294-295) each push a `past` snapshot, so "make it red and 25% bigger" costs two Ctrl+Z presses with a visible intermediate state — the one-entry-per-intent doctrine (S56-A/S62-A) violated for this interleaving (the honest count correctly reads ONE; the pre-apply Revert snapshot covers full recovery, so impact is undo granularity only).
- [Low] The session-79 commit message claims "session_117.md + the worklog entry", but worklog.md was never updated — its last entry is still `Task ID: digma-session-78` (worklog.md:1214) and `git show 32c913b --stat` (70 files) contains no worklog.md. Documentation-honesty class (a pushed claim with no artifact).
- [Informational] The multi-selection dashed box's geometry spans HIDDEN members: canvas.tsx:96-100 computes `selectionBounds = boundsOf(selected)` on the UNFILTERED selection, and the S79-E gate (canvas.tsx:563) only requires `selected.some(el => el.visible)` — a mixed-visibility selection's box extends over blank canvas where hidden members sit. This is the documented mixed-case behavior ("an all-hidden selection renders no box"); the box is pointer-events-none + aria-hidden; cosmetic residue.
- [Informational] The Components panel's "+ New component" button (components-panel.tsx:23-30) is a dead control — no onClick, no feedback. Documented scope cut (PAD §10) and reference parity (the reference's own button is a no-op); listed for the dead-control record.
- [Informational] layers-panel rows are unmemoized — a drag tick replaces the elements array identity and re-renders every row (layers-panel.tsx:115 `[...elements].reverse().map(...)`); S75-E gated the panel MOUNT below md and the session-49 perf probe measured 60fps at 120 elements, but at the ELEMENT_LIMIT ceiling (2000) the per-tick O(n) row reconciliation has no measured posture. Unmeasured — posture note only, not a defect claim.

Stage Summary:
- 0 Critical / 0 High / 1 Medium / 3 Low / 3 Informational. The single most important finding: the S79-B swap-boundary flush still loses an edit that lands while a flush is already in flight (the machine-idle case the slice fixed is the common one, but the in-flight + GET-beats-PUT interleaving silently drops the newer outgoing edit — unpinned by the session-79 e2e, which only fires the swap with the machine idle).
- S79-A assessment: CORRECTLY IMPLEMENTED as documented (boardEpoch incremented only by loadProject at editor-store.ts:179; the load-aware transcript exemption, the scopeEpoch revert belt, and the mid-flight epoch refusal all verified in source and pinned by tests) — with the Low residual that an ""→"" load keeps the stale transcript (the reset keys on projectId only) and renders a belt-defused, silently no-op Revert.
- S79-B assessment: CORRECTLY IMPLEMENTED for the documented machine-idle case (firstRunRef mount/swap discrimination, flushNow BEFORE setLoading(true) at editor-view.tsx:1216→1268, the NAMED-outgoing guard, the machine's own swap-guard drop, no double-PUT vs the unmount cleanup) — with the Medium residual race above.
- S79-E assessment: CORRECTLY IMPLEMENTED exactly as documented (HexColorRow's `onBlur={() => setDraft(value ?? "")}` at properties-panel.tsx:315 covers both the swallowed Canvas-Background null clear and the abandoned invalid draft; the single-selection chrome gates on `selected[0]!.visible` at canvas.tsx:498 and the multi box on `selected.some(el => el.visible)` at canvas.tsx:563) — with the Informational residue that the multi box's bounds still span hidden members.

Live probe on the current build: an exported HOSTNAME=unreachable-host-xyz fails the server boot outright; a resolvable container hostname (Docker ALWAYS exports HOSTNAME) binds a non-loopback address so the webServer's localhost:3100 health URL never answers. Findings:
- MEDIUM — editor-view.tsx:507-513 — the machine-carries skip branch records NO leave-transport: when the user exits while the machine's own PUT₁ is in flight with no newer edit (machineCarriesThisState true — exit()-inside-the-800ms-save window), the cleanup skips its PUT₂ AND leaves leaveTransportFor null, so a same-project re-entry mount's GET awaits NOTHING and can answer BEFORE PUT₁ lands (slow PUT: a large board's full-list replace, a slow uplink). loadProject then replaces the store's still-correct element list with the pre-edit server state and stamps it "saved"; the disposed machine drops PUT₁'s healing response; the next local edit's full-list PUT permanently deletes the pre-exit edit server-side — the exact S85-A GET/PUT race, surviving in the one branch the registry never covered. Fix: in the skip branch register `leaveTransportFor = { projectId: state.projectId, done: softLeaveDescriptor.flightDone }` (the machine's surviving flight IS the transport; the registry's done already accepts a bare promise). Secondary edge of the same hole: the single-slot one-shot drain means an intervening OTHER-project mount consumes the slot without awaiting, so a same-project re-entry through a third project also loses the await.
- LOW — canvas.tsx:295-304/:317-323 + editor-store.ts:339-355 — the canvas gesture family (draw commit, moveElements' accumulated dx/dy, the resize write-back) writes x/y/width/height with NO bound: the last unclamped consumers of the server's clampNumber(±100000 / 0..100000) row-builder bounds. An element dragged (or drawn after panning) past ±100000 canvas units (reachable in ~5-10 max-zoom-out drags: 2000px screen at zoom 0.1 = 20000 units each) renders locally, then visibly teleports to the clamped bound when the store-replacing PUT lands — the S84-B/S86-B teleport family's canvas member. Fix: route the draw commit + resize write-back through clampPositionField/clampSizeField, and clamp the accumulated position in moveElements.
- LOW — properties-panel.tsx:139-148 (NumberField) and :231-238 (GuardedNumberInput) — the blur restore fires only for EMPTY/unparseable drafts; a PARSEABLE-but-clamped draft never resyncs when the consumer clamp maps it back to the field's CURRENT value (type 500000 into an X field already at 100000 → clampPositionField returns 100000 → the external value never changes → the render-time compare never fires → the field displays 500000 indefinitely while the model/canvas/server hold 100000; same at rotation/opacity/W/H bounds). HexColorRow's S79-E blur-restore (`setDraft(value ?? "")`) already closes the color twin; the numeric family lacks the same restore. Fix: on blur, resync the draft from the committed model value whenever `Number(draft) !== value` (not only when unparseable).

Stage Summary:
- 0 Critical / 0 High / 1 Medium / 2 Low / 0 Informational; gates re-proven green in my own environment (1076/1076 unit across 145 files, tsc clean, eslint clean over the client tree).
- S86-A (leave-transport ordering): CORRECTLY IMPLEMENTED — flightDone on the descriptor (created beside the assignment before setSaving, resolved in the flush's single finally), PUT₂ chained strictly after the machine's flightDone, the registry's done carrying the whole sequence, the mount's one-shot same-project await; the machineCarriesThisState skip and the pending re-run traced harmlessly (identical payloads). The Medium above is the SEPARATE unregistered-flight hole in the skip branch — the ordering fix is sound.
- S86-B (AI apply clamp family): CORRECTLY IMPLEMENTED — clampText(x, 2000) at both apply sites (add :189, update :230) and clampSizeField on both scaleElements products (editor-store.ts:322-323); the whitespace-only→null contract matches the server's clampText round-trip.
- S86-C (docs honesty): VERIFIED — the six E2E_BASE_URL readers re-derived by my own grep match .env.example's "six"; README's "5 attempts" matches MAX_VERIFY_ATTEMPTS = 5; the PAD §11 live-derived row pins green in the gate run.

Findings:
- LOW (doc-honesty, the S86-C repair's missed twin): tests/e2e/reset-password.spec.ts:137 — the inline comment still says "the same six-call budget in this file's own XFF bucket" while the SAME file's header (:15, the site S86-C/B86-I1 repaired) says "seven auth calls". The session-86 repair corrected the header and left the inline twin; the doc-lows-s86 pin's negative assertion (/six auth calls/) cannot see the hyphenated "six-call" form, so the live pin stays green while the stale claim survives — the doc-claim-as-second-copy hazard recurring INSIDE the repaired defect's own file. Zero runtime impact (the bucket holds 7 of 10, 3 headroom, under XFF either way). Fix: re-anchor :137 to the seven-call form and widen the pin's negative regex to /six.call/i so both twins are covered.
- LOW (test-infra budget honesty, the F68 drift recurrence): tests/e2e/session67-fixes.spec.ts:148 — "never the shared ai:unknown bucket the other specs' three sends live under" claims THREE shared-bucket assistant sends; the call-level enumeration finds NINE (editor-panels.spec.ts ×4 — the askAssistant helper invoked at :1047/:1080/:1106 plus the direct send at :1134; workspace.spec.ts:126; session78-fixes.spec.ts:38; session79-fixes.spec.ts:48 and :114; session80-fixes.spec.ts:152 — all in XFF-less contexts, all keying ai:unknown). Sessions 78/79/80 added six sends the session-67 comment never counted. 11 headroom remains under the 20/5min ceiling (no failure today — the sends also spread across the suite's windows), but the comment is the ONLY documentation of that budget: the next spec author trusting "three sends" believes 17 headroom where 11 exist, and the failure mode when a window crosses 20 is the nastiest class — a mid-suite 429 RATE_LIMITED envelope with no reply, so every deterministic-fallback assertion ("action(s) performed") times out at 15s. Fix: re-anchor to nine (or make it live-derived — the doc-lows-s86 reader-count form grepping the specs' send interactions).
- INFO: clampNumber's null coercion (src/lib/validation.ts:33-37 through buildElementRow at src/lib/editor.ts:903-904) — an explicit JSON null reaches the non-number branch and Number(null) === 0, so a scripted `{"type":"rectangle","width":null}` stores width 0 where undefined takes the documented fallback 100 — the seam's undefined-vs-null distinction is silent. API-consumer-only (the client always sends numbers); same family as the standing visible/locked truthiness INFO (audit-28's B-8).
- INFO (standing, re-verified, documented in the session-86 deferred queue): the resend-otp (:69-72) and login-unverified (login :69) unconditional writes can strand inert verifyCode/verifyAttempts residue on a row a concurrent verify-otp just verified (unusable — verify-otp:52's verified gate); the LLM await carries no server-side timeout (ai-assistant/route.ts:95-101 — maxDuration is serverless-only; the queued B82-I7 row with its "verified absent" SDK note).

Findings:
- A91-L1: Low — the `editor-*` dark-chrome convention has ZERO adopters while three doctrine sites claim it (the doc-claim-as-second-copy class, F77(a) applied to a conventions bullet): AGENTS.md's Conventions section ("Editor colors are `editor-*` Tailwind utilities … use them for the dark chrome instead of re-typing `#0d1117` hex"), CLAUDE.md:64 ("Editor chrome uses the `editor-*` utilities …, not raw hex"), and digma_SKILL.md ("Use the … utilities (`--color-editor-*` tokens) — never re-type the hex") all instruct the token utilities — but grep finds ZERO consumers (`bg-editor-|border-editor-|text-editor-` → 0 hits repo-wide) while the tokens sit defined-but-unconsumed in globals.css:40-43 and the ACTUAL chrome is ~49 raw arbitrary-hex sites across the editor components (editor-view 23, properties-panel 13, layers-panel 4, ai-assistant 4, canvas 3, toolbar 1, components-panel 1 — `bg-[#0d1117]`/`bg-[#161b22]`/`border-[#30363d]`). Zero visual impact today (both forms resolve to the same hex), but the hazard is the S75-A palette-pins family inverted: pinned-but-unconsumed tokens plus a doctrine that says the utilities are the way — a maintainer following the documented convention writes utilities no pin covers, and a future @theme token edit silently fails to propagate to the 49 raw-hex sites (the exact drift the convention exists to prevent); the next maintainer also cannot tell which form is canonical. Fix shape: either migrate the 49 sites onto the utilities (a mechanical sweep + the visual-parity screenshot re-capture), or amend the three doctrine sites to the honest record (tokens defined for the dark chrome; the current surfaces use the measured literal hex) and install a one-line coverage pin so the claim can never outrun the tree again. Verified.
- A91-I1: Informational — the canvas's rendered element divs carry aria-label with no role (canvas.tsx:735-736, `aria-label={element.name ?? element.type}` on a plain div inside the role="application" container): ARIA-in-HTML prohibits aria-label on generic elements, so the accessible name computes only incidentally and is dropped by most AT — the elements are non-interactive (interaction flows through the container's pointer handlers) and the layers panel carries the per-element accessible tree, so the practical exposure is minimal, but the attribute is non-conforming as authored (the e2e locators that lean on it keep working — it is also exposed via data-element-id). Posture row, not remediated this session. Verified.
- A91-I2: Informational — the create-project dialog's four template preview images are the client layer's only EXTERNAL content dependency: hardcoded Unsplash URLs in TEMPLATE_META (src/lib/editor.ts:223-244) rendered through a plain <img> (project-card.tsx:776-781). The chrome is reference-parity-documented (the reference serves Unsplash previews, measured), so the deviation is deliberate — but the observation for the deferred queue: an offline/restricted-network deployment renders four broken preview images in the dialog (the dialog itself keeps working), and the user's client contacts a third-party CDN on dialog open, in a repo whose posture is otherwise self-hosted/self-contained (the OAuth/AI/email families). Fix shape if ever desired: inline SVG placeholder previews (the logo.tsx precedent) or a documented availability note. Verified.
